Initially she coped by doubling down on her usual meticulous routines—late nights refining CAD models, weekend troubleshooting of 3-D printed components, and volunteering for extra tasks to prove herself. Over time, the workload and a perfectionistic internal standard eroded her boundaries: she stopped running with her track club, skipped board-game nights, and began spending most evenings alone in her apartment reviewing work in her head instead of resting. When she started to notice that she was no longer enjoying her technical hobbies and that she was making small but consequential mistakes at work because she could not focus on even simple schematics, her manager suggested the employee assistance program, which led to a psychiatric referral.\n- Chief Complaint: “I used to be able to focus for hours on designs, but now my mind feels foggy and I can’t make even basic decisions at work.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I’ve become convinced that the mistakes in my CAD files have set off a chain of failures on the project, and that my boss and the client are quietly gathering evidence to hold me personally responsible when something catastrophic eventually happens, even though people keep telling me the errors were minor and already fixed.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Every so often when I’m alone in my apartment or sitting at my desk late, I hear this calm, almost clinical voice in my head telling me things like, “You ruined the project, you don’t deserve this job, you should be punished,” and it sounds so real that I find myself looking around to see who said it.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to get genuinely excited about tinkering with 3-D printers or going on long runs with my track group, but for months now those things just feel flat and pointless, and I end up sitting on my forever, I just stare at parts before picking them up, and even coworkers have asked if I’m okay because I move and react so slowly in the shop.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): Most days I feel like a complete fraud who slipped through the hiring process, and I lie awake replaying every tiny oversight at work, convincing myself that I don’t deserve my degree or my job and that everyone would be better off if I weren’t dragging the team down.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Where I used to be able to zone in on a design problem for hours, now my brain feels like it’s full of static—I reread the same line on a drawing five times, can’t decide something as simple as which tolerance scheme to use, and even picking what to eat for dinner can paralyze me for fifteen minutes.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On bad nights, when I’m lying there thinking about how I’ve supposedly ruined everything, I catch myself wishing I just wouldn’t wake up, and I’ve even found myself picturing in detail what it would be like to drive my car off the overpass on my commute, even though I haven’t acted on it.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_major_depressive_episode: Those thoughts that my boss is secretly building a case against me and the harsh voice telling me I should be punished only started after I slid into this foggy, joyless state last year, and on the rare days when my mood and energy feel closer to normal, that paranoid feeling and the voice pretty much disappear.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable people on the team to missing internal deadlines, needing coworkers to double-check simple drawings, and turning down almost all social plans because I’m either too exhausted or too wrapped up in worrying about work to function normally outside the job.\n- duration: Ever since a few months after I started the full-time position on this aerospace project—so for a little over a year now—I’ve felt this heavy depression with the guilt, foggy thinking, and scary thoughts about being to blame better off if I weren’t dragging the team down.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Where I used to be able to zone in on a design problem for hours, now my brain feels like it’s full of static—I reread the same line on a drawing five times, can’t decide something as simple as which tolerance scheme to use, and even picking what to eat for dinner can paralyze me for fifteen minutes.\n- S032 disappear.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable people on the team to missing internal deadlines, needing coworkers to double-check simple drawings, and turning down almost all social plans because I’m either too exhausted or too wrapped up in worrying about work to function normally outside the job.\n- duration: Ever since a few months Initially she coped by doubling down on her usual meticulous routines—late nights refining CAD models, weekend troubleshooting of 3-D printed components, and volunteering for extra tasks to prove herself. Over time, the workload and a perfectionistic internal standard eroded her boundaries: she stopped running with her track club, skipped board-game nights, and began spending most evenings alone in her apartment reviewing work in her head instead of resting. When she started to notice that she was no longer enjoying her technical hobbies and that she was making small but consequential mistakes at work because she could not focus on even simple schematics, can’t make even basic decisions at work.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I’ve become convinced that the mistakes in my CAD files have set off a chain of failures on the project, and that my boss and the client are quietly gathering evidence to hold me personally responsible when something catastrophic eventually happens, even though people keep telling me the errors were minor and already fixed.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Every so often when I’m alone in my apartment or sitting at my desk late, I hear this calm, almost clinical voice in my head telling me things like, “You ruined the project, you don’t deserve this job, you should be punished,” and it sounds so real that I find myself looking around to see who said it.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I forever, I just stare at parts before picking them up, and even coworkers have asked if I’m okay because I move and react so slowly in the shop.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): Most days I feel like a complete fraud who slipped through the hiring process, and I lie awake replaying every tiny oversight at work, convincing myself that I don’t deserve my degree or my job and that everyone would be better off if I weren’t dragging the team down.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Where I used to be able to zone in on a design problem for hours, now my brain feels like it’s full of static—I reread the same line on a drawing five times, can’t decide something as simple as which tolerance scheme to use, and even picking what to eat for dinner can paralyze me for fifteen minutes.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On bad nights, when I’m lying there thinking about how I’ve supposedly ruined everything, I catch myself wishing I just wouldn’t wake up, and I’ve even found myself picturing in detail what it would be like to drive my car off the overpass on my commute, even though I haven’t acted on it.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_major_depressive_episode: Those thoughts that my boss is secretly building a case against me and the harsh voice telling me I should be punished only started after I slid into this foggy, joyless state last year, and on the rare days when my mood and energy feel closer to normal, that paranoid feeling and the voice pretty much disappear.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable people on the team to missing internal deadlines, needing coworkers to double-check simple drawings, and turning down almost all social plans because I’m either too exhausted or too wrapped up in worrying about work to function normally outside the job.\n- duration: Ever since a few months after I started the full-time position on this aerospace project—so for a little over a year now—I’ve felt this heavy depression with the guilt, foggy thinking, and scary thoughts about being to blame drawings, and turning down almost all social plans because I’m either too exhausted or too wrapped up in worrying about work to function normally outside the job.\n- duration: Ever since a few months It is definitely heavier than just a low-level background depression, as it's exhausting and has seriously messed up my ability to do my job and see friends. But it isn't a total inability to function either, since I'm still dragging myself to work every day and trying to get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your symptoms feel more like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud that has been present for the entire year, or have there been distinct episodes where your mood dropped significantly lower than your usual state over the past twelve months?"

It’s incredibly frustrating because my job as an engineering technician requires so much precision, and right now I just feel completely stuck.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar periods of feeling this stuck and unable to focus in the past, or is this the first time you have felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is the first time I've ever felt like this. I used to be very focused and active, but this heavy, foggy feeling and the inability to make decisions started about a year ago, a few months after I transitioned into my full-time engineering role.\n\nDoctor: Did this change in your mood and focus begin specifically in response to a particular stressful event or adjustment related to your new role?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really started when I transitioned from an internship to a full-time role on a high-visibility aerospace project with tight deadlines and a demanding boss. I tried to cope by working late nights and weekends to meet my own high standards, but the constant pressure eventually wore me down completely. That's when I started losing my focus and slid into this heavy, foggy state.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the difficulty with focus and decision-making at work, have you been experiencing other symptoms like a persistent low mood, loss of interest in hobbies, changes in sleep, or feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like tinkering with 3D printers or running, and they just feel totally pointless now. I also feel like a complete fraud who doesn't even deserve my degree or this job. I lie awake at night for hours just replaying all my mistakes, feeling like I'm constantly dragging my whole team down.\n\nDoctor: Since these feelings began a year ago, have there been any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and loss of interest been present most of the day, nearly every day, throughout this entire year?\n\nPatient: It has been present almost constantly, nearly every day, for a little over a year now. There are only very rare, brief days where I feel slightly closer to my normal self. On those rare days, the paranoid feelings and that harsh voice pretty much fade away too.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been any stretches of two months or longer where you felt completely free of these symptoms, or has the low mood and loss of interest been present almost every single day?\n\nPatient: No, I definitely haven't had any stretches of two months where I felt normal. This heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been present almost every single day for over a year now. At most, I might have a single day where things feel slightly lighter, but it never lasts.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current state as a constant, severe sense of hopelessness and total inability to function, or is it more of a persistent, low-level 'background' depression that you have learned to live with over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'd say my experience is somewhere in the middle of those two. It is definitely heavier than just a low-level background depression, as it's exhausting and has seriously messed up my ability to do my job and see friends. But it isn't a total inability to function either, since I'm still dragging myself to work every day and trying to get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your symptoms feel more like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud that has been present for the entire year, or have there been distinct episodes where your mood dropped significantly lower than your usual state over the past twelve months?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud hanging over me most days for a little over a year, rather than separate, distinct episodes. While there are rare days when my mood and energy feel slightly closer to normal, the heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been pretty much constant since I transitioned to this full-time position.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and loss of interest, have you experienced any physical changes like a significant change in your appetite, weight, or a noticeable slowing down of your physical movements and speech?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel like my body is constantly moving through molasses, and coworkers have even asked if I'm okay because I move and react so slowly now. It takes me forever just to walk from my car to the lab or to pick up parts. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite or weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods in the last year where your symptoms were so severe that you felt completely unable to function at work or home for two weeks or more, or has the intensity remained relatively consistent throughout the year?"

I lie awake at night for hours just replaying all my mistakes, feeling like I'm constantly dragging my whole team down.\n\nDoctor: Since these feelings began a year ago, have there been any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and loss of interest been present most of the day, nearly every day, throughout this entire year?\n\nPatient: It has been present almost constantly, nearly every day, for a little over a year now. There are only very rare, brief days where I feel slightly closer to my normal self. On those rare days, the paranoid feelings and that harsh voice pretty much fade away too.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been any stretches of two months or longer where you felt completely free of these symptoms, or has the low mood and loss of interest been present almost every single day?\n\nPatient: No, I definitely haven't had any stretches of two months where I felt normal. This heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been present almost every single day for over a year now. At most, I might have a single day where things feel slightly lighter, but it never lasts.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current state as a constant, severe sense of hopelessness and total inability to function, or is it more of a persistent, low-level 'background' depression that you have learned to live with over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'd say my experience is somewhere in the middle of those two. It is definitely heavier than just a low-level background depression, as it's exhausting and has seriously messed up my ability to do my job and see friends. But it isn't a total inability to function either, since I'm still dragging myself to work every day and trying to get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your symptoms feel more like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud that has been present for the entire year, or have there been distinct episodes where your mood dropped significantly lower than your usual state over the past twelve months?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud hanging over me most days for a little over a year, rather than separate, distinct episodes. While there are rare days when my mood and energy feel slightly closer to normal, the heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been pretty much constant since I transitioned to this full-time position.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and loss of interest, have you experienced any physical changes like a significant change in your appetite, weight, or a noticeable slowing down of your physical movements and speech?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel like my body is constantly moving through molasses, and coworkers have even asked if I'm okay because I move and react so slowly now. It takes me forever just to walk from my car to the lab or to pick up parts. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite or weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods in the last year where your symptoms were so severe that you felt completely unable to function at work or home for two weeks or more, or has the intensity remained relatively consistent throughout the year?\n\nPatient: The intensity has been relatively consistent throughout the year rather than hitting me in isolated two-week blocks. It has been a constant, ongoing struggle where I am always missing deadlines and withdrawing from my friends. The heavy depression, foggy thinking, and guilt have just hung over me almost every day since I started this role.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or have you ever felt that you would be better off dead?"

I tried to cope by working late nights and weekends to meet my own high standards, but the constant pressure eventually wore me down completely. That's when I started losing my focus and slid into this heavy, foggy state.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the difficulty with focus and decision-making at work, have you been experiencing other symptoms like a persistent low mood, loss of interest in hobbies, changes in sleep, or feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like tinkering with 3D printers or running, and they just feel totally pointless now. I also feel like a complete fraud who doesn't even deserve my degree or this job. I lie awake at night for hours just replaying all my mistakes, feeling like I'm constantly dragging my whole team down.\n\nDoctor: Since these feelings began a year ago, have there been any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and loss of interest been present most of the day, nearly every day, throughout this entire year?\n\nPatient: It has been present almost constantly, nearly every day, for a little over a year now. There are only very rare, brief days where I feel slightly closer to my normal self. On those rare days, the paranoid feelings and that harsh voice pretty much fade away too.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been any stretches of two months or longer where you felt completely free of these symptoms, or has the low mood and loss of interest been present almost every single day?\n\nPatient: No, I definitely haven't had any stretches of two months where I felt normal. This heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been present almost every single day for over a year now. At most, I might have a single day where things feel slightly lighter, but it never lasts.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current state as a constant, severe sense of hopelessness and total inability to function, or is it more of a persistent, low-level 'background' depression that you have learned to live with over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'd say my experience is somewhere in the middle of those two. It is definitely heavier than just a low-level background depression, as it's exhausting and has seriously messed up my ability to do my job and see friends. But it isn't a total inability to function either, since I'm still dragging myself to work every day and trying to get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your symptoms feel more like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud that has been present for the entire year, or have there been distinct episodes where your mood dropped significantly lower than your usual state over the past twelve months?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like a constant, unrelenting dark cloud hanging over me most days for a little over a year, rather than separate, distinct episodes. While there are rare days when my mood and energy feel slightly closer to normal, the heavy depression, guilt, and foggy thinking have been pretty much constant since I transitioned to this full-time position.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and loss of interest, have you experienced any physical changes like a significant change in your appetite, weight, or a noticeable slowing down of your physical movements and speech?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel like my body is constantly moving through molasses, and coworkers have even asked if I'm okay because I move and react so slowly now. It takes me forever just to walk from my car to the lab or to pick up parts. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite or weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods in the last year where your symptoms were so severe that you felt completely unable to function at work or home for two weeks or more, or has the intensity remained relatively consistent throughout the year?\n\nPatient: The intensity has been relatively consistent throughout the year rather than hitting me in isolated two-week blocks. It has been a constant, ongoing struggle where I am always missing deadlines and withdrawing from my friends. The heavy depression, foggy thinking, and guilt have just hung over me almost every day since I started this role.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or have you ever felt that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yes, on my worst nights, I catch myself wishing I just wouldn't wake up in the morning. I've even found myself picturing in detail what it would be like to drive my car off the overpass on my commute. I haven't actually acted on any of those thoughts, but they are really scary when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Given the intensity of your feelings of worthlessness, the physical slowing of your movements, and these recurring thoughts of suicide, would you say your mood is so severely low that you feel completely empty or unable to experience any pleasure at all, even on those rare days you mentioned?"
  }
]
